In this work, we introduce DistractionBench to evaluate whether VideoLLMs can robustly link subjects and events in the presence of unrelated video segments. Through controlled interventions, such as inserting short advertisement clips into longer videos, we show that VideoLLMs frequently hallucinate interactions between entities from different segments, incorrectly attributing actions from injected advertisements to subjects in the main video. We characterize this systematic hallucination as bag-of-events (BoE) behavior, where models process videos as collections of events rather than temporally structured sequences. Evaluating 11 popular VideoLLMs, we find that all models exhibit substantial BoE behavior. Our findings suggest that VideoLLMs lack reliable mechanisms for temporal grounding and motivate the development of models with more robust subject-event association.
  </details>

- **[S1Arena-MM: Which Multimodal Decisions Can Be Left to System 1?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29773)**  `arXiv:2611.29773`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Lang Lin, Tianyu Chen, Yi Gu, Yang Zhao, Xinran Song, Xinyang Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Perception systems often make discrete decisions: flag a hateful meme, predict whether a pedestrian will cross, detect a spoofed voice, or select the next move in a game. System-1 models, such as OneJev and Jev-Omni, read the media and return probabilities over the options in a single forward pass, while multimodal large language models (MLLMs) generate text. Which multimodal decisions can System-1 models handle, and where do MLLMs perform better? We introduce S1Arena-MM, a benchmark that gives every model the same decision requests and scores the probabilities it returns. It contains 51,060 questions across 94 tasks with image, video, and audio input, drawn from 94 public datasets and three program-generated ones. It also includes 102 closed-loop game environments that a model plays from screenshots; in real-time mode, the game continues while the model decides. We evaluate 33 open models: 12 System-1 models and 21 MLLMs read at the answer token. On static decisions, the best System-1 model leads the best MLLM by 4.9 percentage points on images and is within one point of it on video and audio. Post-training for decisions mainly improves the probabilities: on video, a System-1 model has a lower Brier score than the MLLM that it is post-trained from in 10 of 12 pairs. With up to 2,048 thought tokens per question, the most accurate MLLMs pass the best System-1 models only slightly, and thinking raises the Brier score of most MLLMs. Closed loops separate the models: three System-1 models built on a 27B MLLM reach 22.0-33.3% progression in BALROG, and no other model exceeds 9.7% with thinking off. In real-time GameWorld, latencies of up to 1.1 s per decision cause no measurable loss of progress for System-1 models. Code and data:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Cross-modal RAG: Sub-dimensional Text-to-Image Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21956)**  `arXiv:2505.21956`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Mengdan Zhu, Senhao Cheng, Guangji Bai, Yifei Zhang, Liang Zhao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image generation increasingly demands access to domain-specific, fine-grained, and rapidly evolving knowledge that pretrained models cannot fully capture, necessitating the integration of retrieval methods. Existing Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) methods attempt to address this by retrieving globally relevant images, but they fail when no single image contains all desired elements from a complex user query. We propose Cross-modal RAG, a novel framework that decomposes both queries and images into sub-dimensional components, enabling subquery-aware retrieval and generation. Our method introduces a hybrid retrieval strategy, combining a sub-dimensional sparse retriever with a dense retriever, to identify a Pareto-optimal set of images, each contributing complementary aspects of the query. During generation, a multimodal large language model is guided to selectively condition on relevant visual features aligned to specific subqueries for subquery-aware image synthesis. Extensive experiments on MS-COCO, Flickr30K, WikiArt, CUB, and ImageNet-LT demonstrate that Cross-modal RAG significantly outperforms existing baselines in the retrieval and further contributes to generation quality, while maintaining high efficiency.
  </details>

- **[HighwayVerse: A Chain-of-Inspection Benchmark for Highway Infrastructure Anomaly Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28800)**  `arXiv:2611.28800`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Junyi Yang, Zheng Si, Yilin Zhai, Pengpeng Ouyang, Hongxi Wang, Kangze Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Practical highway infrastructure inspection entails progressive diagnostic reasoning from visual perception to maintenance decisions, yet existing benchmarks remain confined to isolated tasks, leaving Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) insufficiently evaluated across sequential inspection stages. Meanwhile, current datasets focus primarily on highway pavement defects, falling short of real-world inspection across diverse infrastructure components such as highway pavements, guardrails, glare screens, and drainage systems. To address these gaps, we formulate the Highway Infrastructure Inspection Progression Chain (HIIP-Chain), spanning Anomaly Detection, Anomaly Recognition, Condition Analysis, and Maintenance Resolution, and introduce HighwayVerse. HighwayVerse comprises 14,143 real-world Ground and Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) inspection records spanning 100 and 17 anomaly categories, respectively, with 84,858 expert-reviewed question-answer pairs. We evaluate 12 open-source and closed-source MLLMs using the chained metric Final, which credits a correct maintenance decision only when all preceding diagnostic stages are completed correctly. Results show that although models achieve relatively high accuracy when initially judging whether an anomaly is present, their markedly lower Final scores expose struggles across downstream recognition, analysis, and decision-making. This discrepancy indicates that current MLLMs do not fully comprehend specific highway anomaly patterns beyond superficial initial judgments. Furthermore, we propose LaneRAG, a training-free retrieval-augmented framework that provides hierarchical reference guidance achieving consistent performance improvements across diverse MLLMs. Our dataset, evaluation benchmark, and source code will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[CLASP: Deciding Structure and Claims Before Decoding for Whole-Slide Pathology Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28576)**  `arXiv:2611.28576`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Ziaullah Khan, Rashadul Islam Sumon, Md Ariful Islam Mozumder, Duc-Son Pham, Moon-Il Joo, Hee-Cheol_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Whole-slide pathology report generation has advanced through stronger slide encoders, retrieved knowledge, and, recently, slide-level multimodal LLMs. However, these generators leave every report-level choice to the decoder: how long the report is, how it is organised, and which findings it states and where. On the standard PathText-BRCA benchmark, we find that these choices dominate n-gram overlap, as reports from the query's hospital selected without learning approach the state of the art, while decoders under-generate and fall back to the most frequent findings. We present CLASP, a decide-then-decode generator that makes these choices explicitly, once per slide. Before decoding, an Exemplar Structure Prior (ESP) takes the sentence plan and length from reports of image-similar cases, a Calibrated Claim Decision (CCD) selects the findings from calibrated slide posteriors, and a Claim-to-Sentence Placement (CSP) assigns each finding to a planned sentence. A Plan-Conditioned Decoder (PCD), refined by structure-anchored sequence training, then writes the report under these decisions. With 35M parameters, CLASP outperforms retrained report generators and slide-level LLMs of up to 7B parameters, matches the strongest published results, and generalises to lung cancer from unseen hospitals. Its decisions change what the reports state, not only their length, transfer to an LLM decoder, and can withhold what H&E cannot show: withholding receptor status leaves 23% fewer contradictions with clinical records than the same network without decisions.
  </details>

- **[Test-Time Latent Computation to Improve Visual Perception in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30480)**  `arXiv:2611.30480`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Chen Yang, Zheng Fang, Wenqi Fan, Jun Xia_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent work on Large Language Models (LLMs) suggests that reasoning capabilities can be enhanced at test time not only through longer token-level reasoning traces, but also through additional computation in latent space. This raises a natural question for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs): can additional computation in latent space improve visual perception at test time? In this work, we first verify that MLLMs contain stable reusable layers that can be re-forwarded at inference time without disrupting normal generation. Based on this observation, we propose \textbf{Lo}ok \textbf{Ba}ck in the \textbf{L}atent \textbf{S}pace (\textbf{Lobals}), a training-free decoding method that identifies the contiguous layer window with the strongest visual-token attention and re-forwards through it, allowing the model to revisit visual evidence in latent space. We demonstrate that this latent-space revisit strategy can improve MLLMs' performance across diverse multimodal settings, including general vision-language tasks, visual hallucination tasks, and vision-centric tasks.
  </details>

- **[Seeing Is Not Planning: Spatiotemporal Planning and Plan Repair in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28726)**  `arXiv:2611.28726`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Siyuan Li, Ethan Lin, Xiang Li, Tianming Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Planning in a visual world means reading the layout, foreseeing how the state will change, and following the task rules all at once. When a plan fails, a model should also use what went wrong to fix it. We call these two abilities spatiotemporal planning and plan repair, and test them with Railbound-Bench. A model sees a railway puzzle, adds tracks under a budget, and submits one complete layout; a simulator then moves all cars at once and decides whether the layout works. After a failure, the model sees what happened and may submit a new layout, up to five attempts in total. On the 200-puzzle main set, the best of nine models solves 59.5% within five attempts, the next 39.5%, and the other seven at most 10.0%; a 1,000-puzzle extended set with a wider range of difficulty is far easier, at 90.9% for the best model. Controlled studies on 180 fixed puzzles show that knowing the task is not the same as using it. GPT-6.1 Terra copies 97.2% of boards exactly and, given a correct layout as text, predicts all four requested future states in 91.3% of cases, yet only 22.8% of its first layouts work. Planning fails more often as the cars' routes interact, even among puzzles that a search solver finds equally easy. Visual feedback removes the reported fault in 175 of 417 first revisions, but only 43 of these revisions solve the puzzle, and at an equal budget of 40k tokens it does no better than fresh retries. The code and data are open-sourced on GitHub atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Choose What Returns: Object-Aware KV Memory for Selective Recall in Interactive Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28137)**  `arXiv:2611.28137`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Hyunwook Kang, Junoh Park, Hyunggyu Lim, Taesup Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive long-video generation lets users build a story through successive prompts and reuse earlier characters and settings under new instructions. Existing memory methods retain past content, yet they often fail at precise selective recall, returning the wrong appearance of a person, mixing different people, or keeping an unrequested background. Based on our analysis of memory form and retrieval, we introduce an object-aware key--value (KV) memory framework for a frozen autoregressive diffusion generator. It keeps the original KV of tracked object regions, selects background KV outside them by attention-scores, and links each memory with a readable record of its source scene and observed appearance. An LLM selects the requested records, and a memory integrator supplies their KV for generation under the current prompt. We also introduce a six-scene evaluation that tests selective attribute modification, time-specific appearance recall, and multi-object composition, in which each person's requested past appearance changes across requests. Experiments show that our framework more reliably preserves each person's identity while reproducing their requested past appearance than existing memory methods, under both explicit scene references and natural-language cues.
  </details>

- **[MuseBench: Benchmarking Intent-Level Audiovisual Arts Understanding in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30026)**  `arXiv:2606.30026`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuxuan Fan, Gyusik Seo, Jing Hao, Jaemin Cho, Mohit Bansal, Jaehong Yoon_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Audiovisual arts encompass diverse creative disciplines, including cinema, visual arts, stage performance, and game design, where artistic meaning arises from deliberate combinations of visual, auditory, and narrative elements (e.g., fear amplified through claustrophobic framing, or grief conveyed through silence and lingering close-ups). Artistic understanding extends beyond recognizing what is depicted to reasoning about why it is expressed through particular creative choices. Despite progress in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), reasoning about artistic intent remains underexplored, as existing benchmarks largely measure perceptual recognition. To address this gap, we introduce MuseBench, a benchmark for evaluating intent-level artistic understanding in MLLMs. It comprises 4,016 questions derived from video essays spanning cinematic arts, static visual arts, stage performing arts, and game arts. To capture the open-ended nature of artistic analysis at scale, the benchmark combines single-select and variable-option multi-select questions. All questions are generated and refined through a four-phase iterative pipeline combining shortcut filtering, adversarial distractors, and expert validation. We evaluate 34 MLLM versions using either video and audio transcripts (V+T) or video, native audio, and text (V+A+T). The highest observed accuracy is 64.62% (V+A+T), trailing the 87.18% human expert baseline. Further analysis reveals that native-audio models score lowest on game arts, models often recover partial interpretations but struggle to identify complete correct option sets on multi-select items, and adaptive frame-selection models trail the strongest video-specific model. These results identify artistic knowledge and complete interpretation as challenges for audiovisual models.
  </details>

- **[Toward Cognitive Supersensing in Multimodal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01541)**  `arXiv:2602.01541`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Boyi Li, Yifan Shen, Yuanzhe Liu, Yifan Xu, Jiateng Liu, Xinzhuo Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have made rapid progress in open-vocabulary visual understanding, yet they often struggle with cognitive visual reasoning tasks that require maintaining, transforming, and simulating abstract visual states. Existing approaches primarily scale Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in language, forcing inherently spatial or visual computations into discrete text tokens. We introduce Cognitive Supersensing, a training paradigm that equips MLLMs with latent visual reasoning by integrating a Latent Visual Imagery Prediction (LVIP) head that progressively learns refined visual latent states aligned with the evolving reasoning context and target answer, thereby providing a structured visual grounding signal for internal reasoning. We further introduce a reinforcement learning stage that optimizes rationale trajectories using both answer evidence and LVIP-based latent grounding, encouraging reasoning paths that remain visually consistent. To evaluate the cognitive capabilities of MLLMs, we present CogSense-Bench, a comprehensive visual question answering (VQA) benchmark assessing five cognitive dimensions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MLLMs trained with Cognitive Supersensing significantly outperform state-of-the-art baselines on CogSense-Bench and exhibit superior generalization on out-of-domain mathematics and science VQA benchmarks, suggesting that internal visual imagery may be key to bridging the gap between perceptual recognition and cognitive understanding. Causal analyses through noise injection, latent patching, and linear probing show that LVIP states progressively encode task-relevant visual structure and influence downstream answer probabilities, supporting their role as active reasoning signals.
  </details>

- **[Modern Self-Supervised Vision Transformers Are Pareidolic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31238)**  `arXiv:2611.31238`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zihang Lin, Huaiyuan Qin, Liangli Zhen, Qing Guo, Jun Liu, Yew-Soon Ong, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In human perception, pareidolia is the tendency to perceive a meaningful pattern, such as a face, in a random or ambiguousthis http URLfind a similar phenomenon in modern self-supervised Vision Transformers (ViTs): on white noise, which contains neither objects nor spatial structure, a few directions of their features still form spatially coherent patterns across the patches, imposed by the featuresthis http URLcall this behavior pareidolia, and the span of these directions the pareidolicthis http URLnatural images, it holds most of the feature variance in DINOv2 and DINOv3 but little inthis http URLexcess explains why DINOv2 and DINOv3, despite stronger dense features, fall far behind DINO in object emergence, the hallmark ability of self-supervised ViTs to identify the patches of an object on theirthis http URLexperiments further show that scaling down their pareidolic subspaces steadily restores their ability to discover objects, so their object emergence is hindered rather thanthis http URLfindings naturally lead to a training-free calibration: Pareidolia Subspace Calibration(PSC) measures the pareidolic subspace only on a noise input and then calibrates the features of natural inputs by scaling its directions down to the variance level of the remainingthis http URLdiverse object emergence tasks, PSC improves DINOv2 and DINOv3 by up to 31.7 CorLoc on object discovery, 30.2 maxF on saliency detection and 59.9~$\mathcal{J}$ on video objectthis http URLidentify the cause of pareidolia, we trace it block by block and find that it forms in the middle blocks of every self-supervised model we examine but stays strong to the last block only in models trained with a patch-level loss; controlled training confirms that an image-level loss alone lets it fade.
  </details>

- **[WorldLatent: A Unified 4D Latent Space for World Perception and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31209)**  `arXiv:2611.31209`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jingxi Chen, Yujia Chen, Cornelia Fermuller, Yiannis Aloimonos, Wen-Sheng Chu, Du Tran_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative Pre-Training (GPT) represents a profound milestone in natural language processing, serving as the foundation for most of today's state-of-the-art large language models. By casting diverse understanding and generation problems into a shared token representation, GPT enables a single architecture and unified optimization loop to master heterogeneous tasks across massive corpora, giving rise to powerful foundation models for natural language. In contrast, 4D spatiotemporal vision lacks a common representational protocol: perception (such as depth and camera estimation) and generation are either handled by separate one-way pipelines or bolted together from unaligned single-modality spaces. In this work, we introduce WorldLatent, a framework that formulates a unified 4D latent space as a common protocol for both world perception and generation. Rather than relying on fragmented, task-specific models or stacked single-modality representations, our framework proposes a multimodal VAE architecture that compresses and fuses complex 4D visual appearance, geometry, and dynamics into a single structured latent representation. Within this formulation, perception and generation operate as complementary denoising processes over the same latent space, enabling one generative model to learn both capabilities simultaneously within a single training loop. Experiments across representative 4D benchmarks verify that our fused latent protocol achieves specialist-level zero-shot 4D perception, coherent 4D generation, and controllable editing from a single model. By demonstrating that diverse 4D tasks can be seamlessly unified under a shared representational protocol, our work provides a scalable foundation for general-purpose 4D spatial intelligence.
  </details>

- **[OmniV-Bench: Can MLLMs Comprehend Immersive Spatio-temporal Scenes?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31022)**  `arXiv:2611.31022`  `cs.CV`  
  _Liu Yang, Huiyu Duan, Yixuan Gao, Sirui Liu, Lu Liu, Zhaolin Cai, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Omnidirectional videos (ODVs) capture dynamic environments with a full $360^\circ$ field of view, providing rich spatial context and temporal dynamics for applications such as virtual reality (VR), augmented reality (AR), and embodied intelligence. Recent multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made great progress in spatial and temporal understanding, yet their ability to comprehend the immersive 360 spatio-temporal information conveyed by ODVs remains largely unexplored. To bridge this gap, we first introduce OmniV-Bench, a Benchmark designed to comprehensively evaluate the capabilities of MLLMs on spatio-temporal Omnidirectional Video understanding. OmniV-Bench contains 4,123 high-quality ODVs collected from diverse scenes, along with 8,923 human-annotated question-answer pairs spanning 11 fine-grained tasks across three categories, i.e., general, spatial, and spatio-temporal understanding. Extensive benchmark experiments reveal that the spatio-temporal complexity of ODVs poses significant challenges for current MLLMs. To address these limitations, we further propose OmniV-Harness, an agentic harness that enhances ODV understanding through viewpoint-guided evidence initialization and task-relevant evidence acquisition with adaptive tool use. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that OmniV-Harness consistently improves immersive spatio-temporal reasoning ability of MLLMs. Both OmniV-Bench and OmniV-Harness will be released upon publication.
  </details>

- **[Optimising VLM Harnesses to Localise Errors in Generated Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30673)**  `arXiv:2611.30673`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ankit Yadav, Nikhil Cherian Kurian, Arpit Garg, Lingqiao Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Identifying subtle errors in generated videos is an important step toward automated video generation, where errors must be detected to assess and refine generated clips. Recent work shows that pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) can localise such errors, but their effectiveness depends strongly on the workflow used: choices about which frames to inspect, which tools and model calls to make, and how to combine predictions can lead to substantially different results. We study VLM harnesses for video error localisation. Instead of relying on manually designed harnesses, we propose Harness Search and Merge (HSM), an algorithm that automatically constructs them by searching over executable evaluation programs. An LLM orchestrator refines candidate harnesses independently on random training subsets, records findings in a memory, and constructively merges complementary candidates. We evaluate HSM on Spotlight, a recently proposed benchmark for localising errors in generated videos. HSM achieves 0.234 +- 0.012 F1, exceeding the strongest benchmark strategy (0.173 +- 0.016), and retains its lead on an unseen video generator (0.213 +- 0.008 versus 0.140 +- 0.003). On IPV-Bench, it achieves strong localisation performance with lower false-alarm rates than existing optimisation baselines. Together, these results show that searching over complete VLM harnesses can outperform existing evaluation strategies, transfer to an unseen video generator, and reduce false alarms on real videos.
  </details>

- **[SceneCast: Learning Indoor Scene Synthesis from Curated Synthetic Videos with LLM Reward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30345)**  `arXiv:2611.30345`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ziyang Zong, Canming Xia, Haoran Xu, Guang Tan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D indoor scene synthesis requires generating objects to form a complete and coherent scene. Existing approaches rely heavily on manually annotated datasets, leading to high costs and limited generalization. We present SceneCast, an annotation-free framework that trains on large-scale, unlabeled videos, addressing two key challenges: (1) Videos provide only partial views of a scene, requiring the model to extrapolate unseen 3D regions, which is inherently error-prone; (2) Raw videos available online are uncurated, suffering from uncontrolled conditions and variable data quality. First, we design a Transformer-based model that predicts objects sequentially and is fine-tuned with reinforcement learning (RL), where an LLM acts as a spatial common-sense evaluator. The LLM provides reward signals through chain-of-thought reasoning and code-executable geometric checks to refine object configurations. Second, we introduce \trim, a data curation approach that generates training videos with controllable video generation models. Crucially, it retains only high-quality prefix clips via multi-criteria quality assessment with an entropy-based weighting strategy. Evaluated on curated synthetic video sets, our annotation-free approach outperforms state-of-the-art supervised methods in scene quality.
  </details>

- **[ToWo-VTON: Take-Off Wear-On Virtual Try-On via Topology-aware Decoupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30203)**  `arXiv:2611.30203`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhenchen Wan, Tianxi Chen, Runqi Lin, Tianyi Bo, Ce Chen, Jiaxin Huang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Virtual Try-On (VTON) aims to synthesize garment transfer without physical trials. Although recent methods perform well on upper-body garments, they still struggle in lower-body and full-body scenarios with complex occlusions and large silhouette changes. To address this limitation, we introduce ToWo (Take-Off Wear-On), a two-stage paradigm that decouples body structure estimation from garment synthesis. Take-Off first generates a tight-fitting body proxy that provides estimated structural guidance, and Wear-On then synthesizes the target garment conditioned on this prior. This decoupling reduces source-garment interference, supporting garment placement under substantial silhouette variations. Built on this paradigm, we propose ToWo-VTON, a unified topology-aware framework with structure-aware supervision. We construct the Body-Fitting DressCode (BFDC) dataset via body-aware diffusion and VLM-based filtering to provide body-proxy supervision across upper-, lower-, and full-body cases. We instantiate ToWo-VTON on both diffusion-based and MLLM-guided backbones. For diffusion-based models, we fine-tune on BFDC using the body proxy as the person input. For the MLLM-guided model, we use native backbone conditioning for the body proxy and target garment, and add Garment Boosting Attention (GBA) to preserve garment details. Experiments on VITON-HD and DressCode demonstrate improvements over the evaluated baselines, with KID reductions of 26.1% and 58.6% for diffusion-based and MLLM-guided implementations, respectively. The combined BFDC-training and Take-Off/Wear-On strategy also produces more coherent limb layouts and garment boundaries across the evaluated garment categories.
  </details>

- **[Visual-RFT++: Extending Visual Reinforcement Fine-Tuning to Multimodal Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30193)**  `arXiv:2611.30193`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ziyu Liu, Zeyi Sun, Xiaoyi Dong, Yuhang Cao, Haodong Duan, Dahua Lin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reinforcement Fine-Tuning (RFT) has recently become a central paradigm for advancing Large Reasoning Models (LRMs), as demonstrated by systems such as OpenAI's o3 and the R1 series. Despite significant progress in LLMs, how to leverage RFT to unify visual perception, step-by-step reasoning, and agentic tool use into one coherent training paradigm remains an open challenge. In this work, we present Visual Reinforcement Fine-Tuning++ (Visual-RFT++), a unified framework that extends RFT to both visual perception tasks and multimodal agentic workflows, enabling LVLMs to reason with images, act upon images, and evaluate their own outputs using verifiable reward. With Visual-RFT++, open-source LVLMs not only show significant gains on diverse visual perception tasks, but also acquire agentic abilities such as web searching for real-time information and code-based image manipulation (e.g., cropping, rotation, and analysis). Across visual perception tasks, including fine-grained classification, few-shot detection, reasoning grounding, and open-vocabulary detection, Visual-RFT++ delivers strong gains over SFT, including a +24.3 percentage points improvement in one-shot fine-grained classification and +21.0 percentage points on COCO's 4-shot setting and +15.4 percentage points on LVIS with around several hundred samples. To evaluate agentic behaviors, we introduce the Multimodal Agentic Tool Bench (MAT) with MAT-Search and MAT-Coding. Visual-RFT++ surpasses its baseline by +18.6 F1 / +13.0 EM percentage points on MAT-Coding and +10.3 F1 / +8.7 EM percentage points on MAT-Search. It also shows gains of 29.3 F1 and 25.9 EM percentage points on multi-hop QA benchmarks such as 2Wiki and HotpotQA, demonstrating strong cross-modal generalization. Visual-RFT++ introduces a data-efficient and reward-driven framework that strengthens both visual reasoning and agentic adaptability.
  </details>

- **[OmniSenMo: A Unified Multisensory Language Model for Generalizable Human Motion Estimation, Understanding, and Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30176)**  `arXiv:2611.30176`  `cs.CV`  
  _Cheng Guo, Qiming Cao, Pu Perry Wang, Pu Wang, Hongfei Xue_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) estimate, understand, and predict human motion, but mainly from RGB cameras. Depth, LiDAR, mmWave, and WiFi complement RGB under poor lighting, through occlusions, and in privacy-sensitive applications, but existing multisensory models cover only some of these sensing tasks. Covering all of them from any sensor subset is challenging, since the tokens of heterogeneous sensors are semantically inconsistent and spatially misaligned, paired multisensory data are scarce, and the tasks need different temporal granularities. We present OmniSenMo, a multisensory language model that takes the human skeleton as a common reference for all sensors. Its skeleton-grounded motion representation turns each sensor into joint tokens, one per body joint, so that any sensor subset is fused joint by joint and expressed in the motion vocabulary that the language model learned from motion capture and text. We pretrain it on all five sensors generated by our multisensory simulator, and Multi-Granularity Observation gives each task the frames it needs. On a cross-action split of MM-Fi, the same model with all five sensors outperforms the models compared on each task, including LEO-Fuse in pose estimation (84.1 vs. 89.0 mm MPJPE) and HoloLLM in unseen-action recognition (69.2% vs. 42.8%). Even without RGB, it outperforms both methods that use RGB, and in a cross-dataset validation on XRF55, it recognizes unseen actions far better than HoloLLM.
  </details>

- **[Multimodal View Training: Learning More Through Different Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30165)**  `arXiv:2611.30165`  `cs.CV`  
  _Devang Kulshreshtha, Priyanka Kaswan, Minh Pham, Chinmay Hegde, Niv Cohen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Can a language model learn more from the same information simply by encountering it in different forms? We answer this in the affirmative by introducing Multimodal View Training (MVT), a simple training approach that presents identical information through text, speech, and images, without requiring new facts or supervision. Starting from a text sample, MVT generates corresponding audio and rendered-text image views, and trains a multimodal language model to produce the same target output across all three views. To further bring these benefits to text-only large language models (LLMs), we train lightweight connector modules to pretrained audio and vision encoders, allowing the LLM to learn from multimodal inputs while retaining its text-only architecture at inference. Across question answering, machine unlearning, and knowledge editing, adaptive MVT improves QA recall by +15.2 percentage points, forget quality by +40.7 points, and editing generality by +25.2 points over text-only training. Combining MVT with standard augmentation further improves QA and editing generality. Our theoretical analysis illustrates how training across modalities can shift weight updates toward shared factual content and reduce their reliance on features specific to each view.
  </details>

- **[UUBench: Understanding the Unspoken toward Full-Duplex Multimodal Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30157)**  `arXiv:2611.30157`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ye Pan, Chenfei Liao, Bin Ren, Hanqian Li, Haocong He, Jialei Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Effective real-time, full-duplex multimodal interaction requires systems to understand not only what users say, but also the communicative needs conveyed through prosody, facial expressions, gestures, and context, and to generate audiovisual responses that appropriately address those needs. Existing evaluations offer limited insight into whether systems translate an understanding of such unspoken needs into effective responses. We introduce UUBench, a benchmark for assessing how well multimodal interactive systems Understand the Unspoken intent and address users' implicit communicative needs. The inherent subjectivity of implicit intent inference poses a key challenge for evaluation. To address this, we introduce Process-Oriented Scoring, which grounds the assessment of intent fulfillment in observable auditory and visual cues, such as prosody and gestures. Rather than relying solely on a single overall score, it evaluates how well a response addresses the communicative needs conveyed by each applicable primitive dimension. We aggregate the dimension-level scores according to each dimension's relevance to the annotated evidence. Beyond intent fulfillment, Outcome-Oriented Scoring evaluates the naturalness, appropriateness, and human-likeness of the reply itself under audio-only, visual-only, and audiovisual conditions. To make these judgments traceable to observable evidence, we propose a Fact-Grounded Scoring JEV module, which uses structured, timestamped audiovisual observations of the user and the digital human's reply to assess one dimension or criterion at a time. UUBench provides a foundation for assessing whether multimodal interactive systems respond effectively to meaning beyond literal wording, supporting progress toward context-aware, real-time, full-duplex interaction.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Binary Correctness: A Surrogate for Confidence Learning in Large Language and Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30059)**  `arXiv:2611.30059`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chen Li, Xiaoling Hu, Chao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Confidence estimation for large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) aims to assess how likely a particular generated response is to be correct. We investigate how learning this assessment can benefit from the model's predictive reliability on the underlying query. Binary correctness supervision, however, assigns identical labels to occasional errors and frequent failures, as well as isolated successes and consistent success. These outcomes can arise from substantially different predictive behavior under stochastic decoding. We propose a confidence-learning framework that supplements realized response correctness with query-level predictive reliability. We estimate reliability through repeated generations under a fixed model and decoding configuration, then combine the empirical success rate with individual correctness to construct a graded confidence surrogate. The surrogate differentiates responses sharing the same correctness label while preserving the average predictive reliability of the sampled response pool. To learn this target, we condition an independent confidence estimator on the query and its full generated response, and optimize backbone LoRA adapters together with a projection and scalar confidence head using mean squared error. Repeated sampling is required only for supervision construction; inference scores an individual response without additional generations. We establish ordering and mean preservation properties of the surrogate and derive a selective-risk bound connecting population surrogate prediction error to the error rate among accepted responses. We evaluate the framework across language and vision-language models, examining confidence calibration and selective prediction.
  </details>

- **[3AGameFactory-Motion: Agentic Rigging and Reliable Complex Motion Generation for Diverse 3D Assets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30013)**  `arXiv:2611.30013`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bohan Zeng, Kaixin Zhu, Yifan Yang, Dongge Yu, Zihao Wu, Yifan Dai, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Animating arbitrary 3D assets from instructions requires both structural inference and motion control, yet existing motion benchmarks mostly cover conventional human or animal skeletons with simple instructions. We introduce DivDynBench, which evaluates rigging and motion generation across six morphology categories, with instructions requiring multi-phase part coordination and explicit timing, angle, repetition, and trajectory constraints. With large language models, we systematically compare three common paradigms: specialized motion generation, video generation followed by motion recovery, and direct agent-driven generation. We analyze their control capabilities, cross-morphology adaptability, and generation costs. Guided by this comparison, we propose an Agentic Motion Framework in which an agent writes compact parametric skeleton and motion programs, and a deterministic executor performs rigging, kinematic solving, and geometric correction. Compact parameter control reduces generation overhead and improves rigging reliability and motion-control accuracy, providing practical guidance for deploying complex 3D motion generation.
  </details>

- **[Towards Agentic Video Corpus Moment Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29877)**  `arXiv:2611.29877`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenwei Xu, Shang Wu, Zhenyu Pan, Jianshu Zhang, Pranav Maneriker, Lie Lu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video corpus moment retrieval (VCMR) requires finding the video and time interval that match a text query in a corpus of videos. We present RELAY, a training-free framework for VCMR that shares memory across queries. RELAY processes a batch of queries in three stages: search with a query bank, ranking with video notes, and localization with video prompt caching. In the search stage, an embedding model scores each video by its best-matching frame. The query bank penalizes videos that score highly for many other queries in the batch. In the ranking stage, a vision-language model (VLM) writes notes on each candidate video's scenes, people, objects, and actions once. Then, a large language model (LLM) reranks the candidates from these notes. In the localization stage, a VLM predicts the time interval of the moment in the top-ranked video. When multiple queries select the same video, prompt caching lets the VLM reuse its frames. On the three VERIFIED benchmarks, RELAY outperforms the trained baselines in video retrieval R@1 without training. Without any labeled moment, its predicted intervals are shorter than the annotated ones. In VCMR, it achieves similar performance to VideoSearch-R1 on one benchmark and trails it at IoU 0.5 on the other two. Human annotators also mark shorter moments than the original annotations of the benchmarks. A single margin, calibrated on a few labeled queries, widens the predicted interval to the annotation convention of the benchmarks. With it, RELAY outperforms the trained baselines in VCMR at IoU 0.5.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Language Optimization: Enhancing Visual Representations for Radiology Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29783)**  `arXiv:2611.29783`  `cs.CV`  
  _Rui Zuo, Qinyue Tong, Ziqian Lu, Zheming Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate chest X-ray radiology report generation (RRG) requires recognizing clinically relevant abnormalities and accurately describing them. Although Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) improves the clinical accuracy of generated reports, our analyses suggest limited improvements in visual representations for abnormality recognition. To address this limitation, we propose a selective regional visual distillation framework that enhances visual representations of challenging abnormalities while preserving existing visual capabilities. Specifically, we train a region-aware teacher with targeted supervision on challenging anatomical regions to acquire complementary visual knowledge. Rather than transferring all teacher features, we verify their utility through regional feature replacement and select only those that improve the original student's performance. We further introduce a Spatially Gated Residual Adapter (SGRA) to distill these verified features while minimizing interference with the student's existing visual representations. Experiments on MIMIC-CXR and IU-Xray demonstrate improved clinical report accuracy and region-level abnormality recognition across different multimodal large language model backbones, while largely preserving performance on non-target findings.
  </details>

- **[DictaRashi: A State-of-the-Art OCR Model For Rashi Script](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29760)**  `arXiv:2611.29760`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ometz Shmidman, Yonah Taurog, Aryeh Sanders, Shlomo Bluth, Moshe Koppel, Joshua Geudalia, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We release DictaRashi, a Tesseract OCR model for scanned Hebrew books printed in the so-called "Rashi" font. We trained DictaRashi on over one million manually corrected lines of text from over 100 different books of Rabbinic and post-Rabbinic literature. On a held-out set of 1,120 manually transcribed lines from scanned Rashi-script pages, DictaRashi reaches a character error rate (CER) of 0.86% and a word error rate (WER) of 3.38%, setting a new state-of-the-art for publicly-available Tesseract models. We provide a full comparison of our results to those of the 31 other publicly available Hebrew Tesseract models; the best of those models achieves a 2.98% CER and 11.86% WER. We further demonstrate that DictaRashi outperforms the current frontier LLMs on this OCR task. We release the test set of Rashi-script documents so that it may be used as a benchmark for future work in this area. We are pleased to release the model and test set to the community under a CC BY NC 4.0 license.
  </details>

- **[Inspect Before You Decide: Evidence-Guided Visual Search for Industrial Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29551)**  `arXiv:2611.29551`  `cs.CV`  
  _Peng Chen, Chao Huang, Yangyang Liu, Maodong Wu, Jinyi Xu, Mingbo Yang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable potential for industrial anomaly detection and understanding. However, reliably identifying subtle anomalies remains challenging, as existing approaches often struggle to acquire and verify fine-grained visual evidence beyond global observations. To address this challenge, we propose EVAD, an evidence-guided visual search framework that enables MLLMs to actively seek and examine diagnostically informative visual evidence for reliable anomaly reasoning. Specifically, EVAD begins with a global visual assessment to identify potentially anomalous regions for further inspection. Building upon this observation, we introduce a Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS)-guided exploration strategy that progressively acquires visual evidence through two complementary operations: semantic refinement for fine-grained inspection and spatial expansion for contextual verification. The acquired evidence is subsequently evaluated and integrated to support more informed and reliable anomaly decisions. Extensive experiments across multiple industrial anomaly detection tasks demonstrate that EVAD achieves state-of-the-art performance in most evaluated settings, highlighting the effectiveness of active evidence exploration for fine-grained anomaly understanding and reasoning. The code will be publicly available upon publication.
  </details>

- **[CAR: Reducing Decoder Output Shift in Visual Token Pruning via Coverage Selection and Attention Reallocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29489)**  `arXiv:2611.29489`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xixi Jiang, Pingcheng Dong, Chen Yang, Xin Yang, Kwang-Ting Cheng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual token pruning is an effective way to accelerate multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, existing methods degrade substantially under aggressive pruning, especially on visual grounding (VG). We analyze the sources of this degradation from the perspective of the output shift that pruning causes in decoder layers, and decompose this shift in closed form into two terms, an image summary error and an attention allocation error. The summary error stems from the loss of visual information, and the allocation error from the drop in the share of attention assigned to visual tokens. To reduce both errors, we propose CAR (Coverage selection and Attention Reallocation), a token pruning framework that restores attention share while maintaining information. Specifically, to minimize the summary error, whose upper bound tightens as the retained tokens better cover all visual tokens, our coverage selection retains tokens that are both salient and non-redundant, with saliency from encoder heads chosen by a label-free probe. To minimize the allocation error, our attention reallocation reassigns the attention of the discarded tokens to the retained ones through a closed-form logit bias. On Qwen2.5-VL-7B with $90\%$ of the visual tokens pruned, CAR retains $93.0\%$ of the unpruned accuracy on visual question answering (VQA) and $77.9\%$ on VG, against $26.5\%$ for the best existing method on VG; the logit bias alone raises the VG accuracy of existing methods by up to $24$ points. Experiments on multiple MLLMs across VQA, VG and video understanding show that CAR generalizes well and consistently outperforms existing methods.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Attention Maps: Understanding and Improving Medical MLLMs via Visual Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29361)**  `arXiv:2611.29361`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yichen Zhao, Fenghe Tang, Wenxin Ma, Rongsheng Wang, Zihao Wan, Shaohua Kevin Zhou_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Current MLLM methods largely focus on whether models attend to the right visual regions, implicitly treating spatial attention map as the primary key of effective visual perception. To examine this assumption, we introduce a medical VQA dataset with paired non-contrast (NC) and contrast-enhanced (CE) images that share corresponding anatomy while providing different cues. We show that this view is incomplete: across paired NC and CE medical images, MLLMs often exhibit similar attention patterns despite producing contrasting answers, while controlled bidirectional interventions induce substantially larger prediction changes on visual values than attention weights. These results identify the visual content retrieved through values as a critical factor in improving perception beyond spatial attention alone. Guided by this finding, we propose VIVID (VIsual Value Intervention Distillation), which uses enhanced visual values to identify and refine teacher corrections aligned with useful visual evidence to improve on-policy distillation from CE to NC images. Across six internal and external test sets, VIVID consistently outperforms existing on-policy distillation methods across different model scales and architectures, requires only NC images at inference, and maintains strong zero-shot performance on general-domain VQA benchmarks. The project page is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[CT-Navigator: Towards Interpretable and Generalizable CT Understanding through Agentic Perception and Tool-Invocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29305)**  `arXiv:2611.29305`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yibing Fu, Huidong Xie, Yihao Niu, Wei Ji, Heming Dong, Ziyue Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Clinical computed tomography (CT) interpretation inherently requires progressive visual exploration, where radiologists navigate from whole scans to individual slices and regions of interest and interact with external tools when necessary. However, existing 3D medical multimodal large language models (MLLMs) typically connect a specialized 3D visual encoder to a language model, without fully leveraging the native 2D visual and agentic capabilities of generalist MLLMs. Trained on large-scale one-hop CT instruction-following data, these models also have limited ability to perform iterative reasoning and explicitly ground their conclusions in visual evidence, limiting their support for traceable CT interpretation. To solve these problems, we propose \textbf{agentic perception} as an alternative paradigm inspired by radiologists' interpretation workflows. We introduce \textbf{CT-Navigator}, a framework that harnesses generalist MLLMs' 2D visual and agentic capabilities to progressively ground CT interpretation in explicit, traceable visual evidence through hierarchical visual exploration and radiology tool invocation. To build these capabilities, we curate 8,904 visual chain-of-thought (V-CoT) trajectories for cold-start agentic supervised fine-tuning. We then optimize the policy through reinforcement learning on 33,219 questions, with data selection and dynamic rollout sampling prioritizing informative learning signals. Finally, we introduce CTNavi-Bench, a 3D CT visual question answering benchmark comprising 7,000 questions spanning anatomical regions throughout the human body. CT-Navigator achieves 79.50\% accuracy on CTNavi-Bench, surpassing the strongest baseline by 17.59\%, and demonstrates strong generalization across three external CT benchmarks. The code, model, and benchmark will be released on \href{this https URL}{GitHub}.
  </details>

- **[MM-DataLoop: Closing the Data-Side Loop for Recursive Self-Improvement in MLLM Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29254)**  `arXiv:2611.29254`  `cs.CV`  
  _Likang Peng, Chen Ju, Xu Chen, Xiangyang Wu, Jinsong Lan, Haihong Tang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recursive self-improvement (RSI) offers a path toward improving models through repeated evaluation and revision. We study its data side in multimodal language model (MLLM) post-training, where heterogeneous operations and cross-round evidence must be coordinated. MMDataLoop organizes data interventions into five operation families and connects them through an Observe-Diagnose-Plan-Execute-Verify-Reflect cycle with shared rule memory. Across 34 candidate rounds on Qwen3.5-0.8B and five benchmarks, we hold the base initialization, shared training schedule and evaluation protocol fixed while varying data recipes. The five-benchmark mean reaches 63.04 at R33, up 5.32 points from the zero-shot baseline; R29, R32, and R33 form a close top cluster within 0.09 points. In eightcandidate comparisons, guided search reaches the preselected target in six candidates and identifies the top observed candidate, while random arms show higher means and lower dispersion. The selected R29 recipe also scores above two full-pool baselines under the same epoch schedule. Together, these findings establish MM-DataLoop as a traceable workflow and provide an empirical basis for studying how data-side decisions shape outcomes across MLLM post-training recipes.
  </details>

- **[Unleashing the Value of Raw Point Clouds for 3D Understanding with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29194)**  `arXiv:2611.29194`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tailai Chen, Jiawei Yu, Yiming Cheng, Ping Nie, Yongyong Chen, Tao Zhou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Standard 3D point cloud language models typically rely on pretrained point encoders to extract 3D features and project them into representations processable by large language models (LLMs). However, these encoders are often originally optimized for pre-defined tasks, resulting in a representation bottleneck that may lead to a substantial mismatch with downstream 3D understanding. Moreover, existing models largely follow a single pass closed-book generation, limiting their ability to actively inspect task-relevant 3D regions or retrieve necessary external knowledge. In this work, we present PointTool, an encoder-free and tool-augmented framework for 3D reasoning. PointTool introduces Alignment-Free Patch Tokenization (AFPT) to locally aggregate raw point clouds while preserving fine-grained geometric structure, and Decoupled Point-Text LoRA (DPL) to facilitate global aggregation and 3D knowledge internalization within a frozen LLM. Building upon these designs, Program-Supervised Tool Reasoning (PSTR) enables the model to explicitly operate on raw 3D data and external resources through executable tools, supporting interactive 3D reasoning grounded in spatial evidence. We further introduce PointCloud-TR, a benchmark comprising 13K 3D objects, 39.8K questions, and 35.6K tool trajectories to facilitate training and evaluation of complex 3D reasoning. Experiments demonstrate that PointTool achieves strong performance on both standard and complex 3D understanding tasks, validating the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
  </details>

- **[Decoupling Perception from Generation for Agentic 3D CT Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29167)**  `arXiv:2611.29167`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenyu Wang, Mingyu Chen, Wenchao Li, Kayhan Batmanghelich_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Chest CT reports repeatedly assess the same anatomical regions for findings and their attributes, such as whether a lung nodule is present and where it is located. These assessments suggest learning to answer visual questions directly, rather than coupling perception to open-ended text generation. Yet current CT report generation models largely follow generic vision-language modeling, which may miss subtle abnormalities when training data are limited. We therefore formulate report generation as a set of visual question-answering decisions coordinated by a large language model (LLM) agent. This decouples visual perception from reasoning and report writing: perception can be learned as a discriminative, non-generative task, while the LLM focuses on reasoning over patient context and posing relevant visual queries. We introduce Rad-VL-JEPA, which unifies report retrieval, finding recognition, and fine-grained attribute characterization through vision-language joint-embedding prediction. Building on this perception module, an agent trained with reinforcement learning (RL) acquires patient-specific visual evidence, while a separate editor incorporates the evidence into the final report, further separating evidence acquisition from report realization. Experiments show that Rad-VL-JEPA achieves strong performance on fine-grained CT understanding tasks with lower amortized per-query inference time than a generative VLM baseline trained with the same CT encoder and VQA data. The resulting reporting system outperforms strong VLM-based and agentic baselines.
  </details>

- **[From Dense to Sparse: Morton-Ordered Sparse Mapping for Efficient 3D Mesh Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28913)**  `arXiv:2611.28913`  `cs.CV`  
  _Junjie Yang, Rui Qian, Yijiang Li, Jinyi Xu, Yuhao Yan, Xinjian Yi, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Unified 3D understanding and generation require compact sequences preserving spatial structure. Dense grids yield long sequences, while serialization can separate spatial neighbors. We propose Sparse Proximity Aware Compression (SPAC), a shared sparse SDF vocabulary combining sparse encoding with reversible spatial merging. A Sparse SDF VQ-VAE quantizes near-surface features while retaining explicit coordinates, using reconstruction supervision for surface fidelity and detail. 3D Byte-Pair Encoding (3D-BPE) merges recurring face-adjacent patterns using relative offsets before serialization. Macro tokens expand exactly into leaf tokens and coordinates. After Morton serialization, a shared LLM reads geometry for captioning and predicts tokens that decode into meshes for text-conditioned generation. With fixed geometry, architecture, and band width in voxels, sparse encoder cost grows approximately quadratically with resolution. 3D-BPE reduces mean complete LLM sequence length by 45.4 percent, including coordinates, text, and control tokens. SPAC achieves strong results in both 3D captioning and text-conditioned mesh generation. Ablations show gains over leaf tokens and Morton 1D-BPE in both tasks. The project page atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Med-Agentic: Distilling Agentic Medical Reasoning with Internalized Meta-Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28176)**  `arXiv:2611.28176`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yucheng Zhou, Junwei Sheng, Jianbing Shen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large language models (LLMs) in expert domains face a trade-off between agentic tool use, which externalizes expertise behind brittle multi-turn runtimes, and end-to-end specialization, which internalizes expertise only implicitly at task-level granularity. We propose internalized meta-capabilities: atomic, parametrically internalized, and composable reasoning units that an LLM can select and combine within a single chain-of-thought (CoT). We instantiate this idea as Med-Agentic for medical visual diagnosis, where the model emits a capability-tagged structured CoT in one forward pass, turning capability invocation into tagged token generation rather than external tool calls. For training, Med-OPD performs multi-teacher on-policy distillation with token-level dual-factor teacher routing conditioned on both the image and the active capability tag, strictly generalizing per-prompt teacher routing. We show that this routing gives an unbiased estimator of the same expected teacher-KL gradient. Across public medical imaging datasets spanning nine anatomies and eight modalities, Med-Agentic consistently improves in-distribution and out-of-distribution diagnostic accuracy over the strong competitors.
  </details>

- **[ReMiC: Representation-Driven Token Pruning for 3D VLMs via Minimax Hierarchical Clustering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28124)**  `arXiv:2611.28124`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bangfeng Yan, Chaoning Zhang, Yuanhao Liu, Pengcheng Zheng, Mengyuan Li, Wei Dong, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D vision-language models (3D VLMs) have demonstrated strong capabilities in 3D scene understanding and spatial reasoning. However, their reliance on multi-view inputs produces long visual token sequences, imposing substantial computational overhead during inference. Existing pruning methods often rely on token salience or feature diversity as proxies for representativeness, without explicitly modeling the representation gap between the original token set and the retained subset, thereby leading to suboptimal representation of the original scene. In this paper, we propose ReMiC, a training-free, representation-driven token pruning framework based on minimax hierarchical clustering. ReMiC treats retained tokens as prototypes for the original observations, formulating pruning as minimizing the maximum representation error under a fixed token budget. Local prototype initialization allocates tokens according to regional representation needs, while global prototype refinement updates the selected prototypes using cross-region information to further reduce the maximum representation error. Extensive experiments conducted on three representative 3D VLMs across five 3D scene understanding benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and generalization of our method. Notably, by retaining only 10% of visual tokens, ReMiC preserves 93.2% of the performance of Video-3D LLM and achieves a $2.04\times$ faster inference speed.
  </details>

- **[PhoneQC: Benchmarking Multimodal LLMs for Order-Level Smartphone Trade-in Inspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28117)**  `arXiv:2611.28117`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zihan Deng, Xinpei Lin, Du Tiantian, Lequan Yu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As vision-language models enter remote smartphone trade-in, sellers already upload six views and a diagnostic screen, which makes it easy to treat the human quote as a step a model can take over. A model may mark a crack or read the screen, yet the quote is a staff form: appearance fill stays near 0.25, and the closed models that score a little higher do so only on the orders they answer, so human appraisal remains necessary. Two obstacles stand in the way. (1) Single-view defect sets and attribute benchmarks do not score an order-level form under a fixed input contract, so a box or a leaked field can be mistaken for a finished quote. (2) The unread half of the form is the staff fields, and a model left to emit boxes or captions does not learn them. We present PhoneQC, a 335-order iPhone-heavy cut that ranks systems by a 0 to 100 form score (Score100) only inside matched protocols (images alone; images plus a detector sidecar), with a declared upper bound that is not ranked as a peer. As a case study on that board, field-only LoRA freezes the backbone, adapts attention projections, and supervises staff fields while leaving the defect list empty; the adapter sees the same images as the zero-shot model. On image-only T1 the 3B model rises from 26.6 to 33.3 at coverage 0.928; without the empty-blocker item the open ranking still favors the adapter, while the remaining gap to a person stays large on this cut.
  </details>

- **[ORDO: Fine-Grained Motion Understanding in Video LLMs via Order-Dependent Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28097)**  `arXiv:2611.28097`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiaye Luan, Chaoning Zhang, Pengcheng Zheng, Dongshen Han, Sheng Zheng, Ping Guo, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video large language models (video LLMs) answer a wide range of questions about real videos, yet they remain weak at fine-grained motion such as whether an object speeds up or slows down. Existing methods train these models on synthetic motion clips, on order pretext tasks such as reordering shuffled clips, or with rewards that compare ordered and perturbed frames. None of them requires that the label be unrecoverable from the frames once their order is discarded, so a model can fit such data from static cues without learning to use frame order. We propose ORDO, a framework that builds this requirement into the training data. We define order-necessary supervision as data in which the unordered set of frames has the same distribution under competing labels, and we realise it for motion attributes with three constructions, time-reversal pairing, matched spatial extent and uniform interval coverage. A procedural curriculum of six motion primitives instantiates these constructions, and low-rank adapters of the language model are trained on it with an answer-level objective and self-distillation replay on real videos. With 4,000 synthetic clips, ORDO raises the mean accuracy over four motion benchmarks by +7.6, +4.9 and +5.1 points on Qwen3.5-9B, Qwen3-VL-8B and Molmo2-8B, while the same training with shuffled frames changes it by less than one point. Code and generator will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[EgoCaptioner: Towards Comprehensive and Fine-Grained Egocentric Video Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26380)**  `arXiv:2611.26380`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuqi Tang, Yang Shi, Yuran Wang, Tengfei Liu, Rundong Wang, Zongyang Qiu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Egocentric video captioning requires fine-grained detail and accurate timing, yet current multimodal large language models (MLLMs) often miss brief actions, confuse their order, or misinterpret the wearer's motion. Existing caption formats also offer limited support for combining detailed narration with precise timing. We present EgoCaptioner, which pairs a video-level overview of the setting, participants, and the wearer's overall activity with timed steps whose narration adapts to varying perceptual demands and marks key moments at specific timestamps. We develop a coarse-to-fine annotation pipeline that establishes event structure before expanding step descriptions, yielding 50,000 training examples from eight egocentric video sources. Complementary rewards assess factual accuracy and coverage, measure step-level temporal alignment, and penalize fragmented or redundant narration, guiding caption refinement through Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO). To improve the reliability of factual supervision, we supplement caption references with pose-derived motion facts when sensor data are available. Across five egocentric benchmarks, EgoCaptioner achieves leading performance among evaluated open-source models in caption quality, caption-conditioned question answering, and temporal alignment. It also surpasses Gemini 3.1 Pro on PhysCapBench and all evaluated proprietary baselines on Minerva-Ego under the caption-to-QA protocol. Ablations support the complementary roles of factual and temporal rewards and show that coarse-to-fine annotation provides more effective supervision than single-pass annotation.
  </details>

- **[Queryable Gaussian View Index: Retrieval, Localization, and Agentic View Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41373)**  `arXiv:2610.41373`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tianyu Xiong, Rui Li, Jiaqi Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Given an existing 3D Gaussian scene and calibrated references, we seek camera poses and views that answer text, RGB, or image-and-instruction queries. RGB localization recovers a source camera; a target-view instruction can require a different camera while preserving the referenced scene content. Queryable Gaussian View Index (QGVI) supports these requests through a semantic view index that binds structured captions and observations to calibrated camera poses. QGVI-T retrieves indexed candidates with BM25. QGVI-A uses this interface and geometric source localization (QGVI-I) as tools, sharing candidate poses, observations, and query conditions marked satisfied, violated, or unknown. Unmet conditions guide camera proposals and rendered checks. Supported answers exit early; unresolved queries return insufficient evidence within a shared search budget. We construct a nine-scene benchmark, including city-scale environments, covering semantic view retrieval, RGB localization with out-of-map rejection, and image-conditioned target-view search. Under GPT assessment on the development cohort, QGVI-A returns confirmed correct views for 90.4\% of target-view queries, compared with 26.8\% for one-shot Retrieve + Select. This comparison evaluates complete policies with different information and call budgets; QGVI-A achieves higher coverage at lower answer precision. The benchmark separates semantic satisfaction, source-camera accuracy, and acceptance decisions.
  </details>

- **[Remote-Sensing City Layout Extraction with MLLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16484)**  `arXiv:2608.16484`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zigan Zhou, Kai Li, Yupeng Deng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Remote-sensing systems usually describe urban content with detection boxes, semantic masks, or vector boundaries. Such outputs locate classes and support image-plane scoring, yet they do not by themselves constitute an executable layout that retains object identities, typed relations, topology, and regeneration rules. Code-as-City instead casts urban-layout extraction from a single top-down image as constrained code generation with a multimodal large language model (MLLM). An image model first produces an aligned five-class semantic layout prior. Three ordered MLLM passes use the image and this prior to recover roads, land-cover regions and relations, and buildings. Deterministic normalization converts the accumulated records into a city graph and a restricted layout program. Executing the program creates a renderable 3D city layout and an orthographic semantic projection over shared geometry. The projection admits pixel-level comparison with remote-sensing masks, while named objects, relations, and editing operations remain available for synchronized regeneration of both views. Evaluated on the 100 scenes of CityLayout-100, the complete framework obtains 41.1% mean intersection-over-union and 48.3% global intersection-over-union. This result provides quantitative evidence that visual observations can be translated into inspectable, editable city code with coupled planar and 3D outputs.
  </details>

- **[PhysAgent: A Multi-Agent Framework for Reliable Remote Heart Rate Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00066)**  `arXiv:2608.00066`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yehui Yang, Bo Zhao, Junzhe Cao, Hui Ma, Yue Sun, Wenjin Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) enables non-contact heart-rate estimation from facial videos, but its weak physiological signal is easily corrupted by motion, illumination changes, occlusion, skin-appearance variation, and device noise. Existing rPPG methods typically rely on a single model to directly predict heart rate or recover pulse waveforms, while different strong estimators may produce conflicting yet individually plausible candidates for the same video. To resolve these conflicts, we propose PhysAgent, an inference-time multi-agent candidate-verification framework. Unlike direct prediction approaches, PhysAgent neither trains a new base rPPG model nor asks Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to output heart rate directly. In contrast, it treats outputs from multiple base estimators as physiological hypotheses to be verified and uses a lightweight 4B MLLM, Qwen3-VL-4B, to drive multi-agent reasoning over video conditions, signal reliability, and candidate disagreement. A deterministic physiological verifier checks the fusion proposal, and a reproducible numerical fusion process produces the final heart rate. Experimental results on multiple public rPPG benchmarks show that PhysAgent improves fusion stability and reliability across different datasets and source-domain settings, while avoiding the irreproducibility and physiological inconsistency of direct MLLM prediction or unconstrained ensemble fusion. The code will be released soon.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Single Expert: Harmonizing Diverse Visual Priors in MLLMs for Spatial Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15054)**  `arXiv:2607.15054`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiao Lin, Xiaohu Huang, Kai Han_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated substantial promise in spatial understanding. Existing works typically incorporate prior knowledge extracted from a pre-trained foundation model to further enhance the spatial awareness of MLLMs. In this paper, we first reveal that when integrating diverse foundation models into MLLMs, different models provide complementary spatial priors that benefit different tasks. Motivated by this, we propose $\textbf{ViPS}$, a novel multi-model prior framework designed to fully unleash the potential of incorporating multiple $\textbf{Vi}$sual $\textbf{P}$riors from diverse models into MLLMs for $\textbf{S}$patial understanding. Specifically, ViPS introduces an Efficient Prior Proxy to generate multiple foundational priors with minimal inference overhead, and a Dynamic Prior Fusion mechanism to achieve harmonious and context-aware prior fusion and injection from the prior proxies. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ViPS successfully harmonizes diverse visual priors, establishing new state-of-the-art performance across multiple complex spatial reasoning and 3D spatial understanding benchmarks. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[SCALER: Breaking the Latent Scaling Bottleneck via Single-Pass Context-Anchored Latent Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12163)**  `arXiv:2605.12163`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenfeng Wang, Wei Zhai, Wei He, Xuhan Zhu, Chunpeng Zhou, Song Yan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Longer chains of thought substantially improve language reasoning on complex problems, inspiring efforts to scale visual reasoning via continuous latents. However, evaluating visual latent reasoning across complexity and sequence length reveals a fundamental **Latent Scaling Bottleneck**: existing methods yield modest gains only within short budgets ($\leq 30$ tokens) on simple tasks, yet stagnate or *degrade* on harder tasks and longer chains. Controlled diagnostics trace this bottleneck to two root causes. First, **Latent Representation Saturation** under autoregressive conditioning causes step-wise cosine novelty to decay exponentially and saturate after $\sim 30$ steps. Second, **Over-Compressed Supervision Targets** from heavily pooled ($\geq 128\times$) image embeddings lose fine-grained spatial structure and act like meaningless padding. We propose **SCALER** (**S**ingle-Pass **C**ontext-**A**nchored **L**at**e**nt Visual **R**easoning), whose lightweight *detransformer* synthesizes spatially aligned auxiliary tokens from full-sequence hidden states *in a single forward pass*, anchoring each token to the original visual space. Coupled with three-stage SFT and **ALPO** (**A**uxiliary **L**atent **P**olicy **O**ptimization) for differentiable RL, SCALER extends effective latent length by over $30\times$, achieves state-of-the-art open-source accuracy (e.g., **57.84%** on MME-RealWorld-Lite, +12.09% over backbone, +10.47% over GPT-6-luna), with strong OOD generalization. Our project page: [this https URL](this https URL).
  </details>

- **[SGR3 Model: Scene Graph Retrieval-Reasoning Model in 3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04614)**  `arXiv:2603.04614`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zirui Wang, Ruiping Liu, Yufan Chen, Junwei Zheng, Weijia Fan, Kunyu Peng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D scene graphs provide a structured representation of object entities and their relationships, enabling high-level interpretation and reasoning for robots while remaining intuitively understandable to humans. Existing approaches for 3D scene graph generation typically combine scene reconstruction with graph neural networks (GNNs). However, such pipelines require multi-modal data that may not always be available, and their reliance on heuristic graph construction can constrain the prediction of relationship triplets. In this work, we introduce a Scene Graph Retrieval-Reasoning Model in 3D (SGR3 Model), a training-free framework that leverages multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) for semantic scene graph generation. SGR3 Model bypasses the need for explicit 3D reconstruction. Instead, it enhances relational reasoning by incorporating semantically aligned scene graphs retrieved via a ColPali-style cross-modal framework. To improve retrieval robustness, we further introduce a weighted patch-level similarity selection mechanism that mitigates the negative impact of blurry or semantically uninformative regions. Experiments demonstrate that SGR3 Model achieves competitive performance compared to training-free baselines and on par with GNN-based expert models. Moreover, an ablation study on the retrieval module and knowledge base scale reveals that retrieved external information is explicitly integrated into the token generation process, rather than being implicitly internalized through abstraction.
  </details>

- **[VLM-UQBench: A Benchmark for Modality-Specific and Cross-Modality Uncertainties in Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09214)**  `arXiv:2602.09214`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenyu Wang, Tianle Chen, Yuxin Wu, Qijia He, Mingyu Chen, H. M. Sabbir Ahmad, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Uncertainty quantification (UQ) is essential for ensuring the safety and reliability of vision-language models (VLMs). A central challenge is to identify the source of uncertainty, whether it arises from the image, the text, or their interaction. We introduce VLM-UQBench, a benchmark for modality-specific and cross-modal data uncertainty in VLMs. It combines human-curated VizWiz subsets covering clean, image-, text-, and cross-modal uncertainty with a scalable perturbation pipeline comprising visual, textual, and cross-modal perturbations. We further introduce two metrics to assess how UQ scores respond to these perturbations and how their changes correlate with hallucinations. Using this framework, we systematically benchmark both LLM-based and VLM-specific UQ methods across nine VLMs and three datasets. Empirically, we find that (i) UQ performance varies substantially across uncertainty sources and VLMs; (ii) modality-specific uncertainty frequently co-occurs with hallucinations, yet changes in UQ scores only weakly track these failures; and (iii) we uncover response-length confounding in UQ evaluation, where seemingly effective uncertainty detectors may rely on simple answer-length cues. These findings highlight limitations in both current UQ methods and their evaluation, calling for more robust, modality-aware UQ approaches and more rigorous evaluation protocols that go beyond detection performance to assess the reliability of uncertainty signals.
  </details>

- **[Lingua-SafetyBench: A Benchmark for Safety Evaluation of Multilingual Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22737)**  `arXiv:2601.22737`  `cs.CV`  
  _Enyi Shi, Pengyang Shao, Yanxin Zhang, Chenhang Cui, Jiayi Lyu, Xiaobo Xia, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The robust safety of Vision-Language Large Models (VLLMs) against joint multilingual and multimodal threats remains severely underexplored.
